Asia EM Equity Strategy | Asia Pacific

# Alpha, Beta, Thematic Biweekly – Stay Bullish Post-NIRP Japan, but Refocus on ROE

We explore the post-BoJ outlook for Japan equities, where we call for some rotation in leadership, with renewed focus on corporate reform and ROE heading into F3/24 results and midterm plans. Also see performance, valuations, flows and positioning metrics inside.

## Key Takeaways

Market Views: Japan and India remain top DM and EM picks, alongside OWs on Mexico, Poland, Greece and Singapore.

– Sector Views: We like Semis/Equipment into an AI boom and hardware recovery as well as Financials in India and Indonesia, and Industrials in India and Mexico.

Key Themes: We like AI Enablers and Adopters, Japanese Corporate Reform, and select China SOE and Korea Value opportunities.

■ Flows and Positioning: Flows picked up in China via passive vehicles with US/EU investors still selling. Japan has seen inflows, but global investors remain UW.

Exhibit 1: Chart of the Fortnight: Average sector returns vs. TOPIX after last three BoJ rate hikes (2001, 2006, 2007); we look for Real Estate and Foods to again outperform in this cycle
![](images/594ab3a2aa8cc028d4b3df07dd80956a6a953e901d33351bfc40529a7062c6cc.jpg)
Source: Bloomberg, Morgan Stanley Research

Top of the Agenda: The BoJ has ended an eight-year phase of negative interest rates and formally dropped its YCC and ETF-buying programs, which were increasingly left on standby. We see this step as confirming the positive wage-price feedback loop now in place, which drives a reflationary dynamic for domestic revenue and potential margin expansion. An orderly FX market reaction leaves Japan's secular bull market intact, with focus now turning to corporate reform implementation at/ after F3/24 results and mid-term plans in May, followed by shareholder meetings in

## Top of the Agenda

Daniel Blake, Kristal Ji

This fortnight we tackle:

• Monetary Policy Normalisation in Japan – Staying Bullish Equities

• Navigating Latest Market Dynamics Amid Crowding Risks

## Monetary Policy Normalisation in Japan – Staying Bullish Equities

The Bank of Japan (BOJ) on March 19 ended an era of exceptional monetary policy accommodation by removing the Negative Interest Rate Policy, abolishing the formal Yield Curve Control mechanism (while maintaining the ability to make ad hoc bond purchases) and ceasing the purchase of equity ETFs and REITs. Policy normalisation was expected by MS economists and consensus.

Most important, the BoJ adjustments are a result of the success of the prior policy regime, in conjunction with fiscal policy and structural reform, in delivering a much-improved macroeconomic environment in Japan. This can be characterised as a virtuous cycle of rising nominal GDP growth, wages, prices and corporate profits. Please see our Blue Paper Revisit: Japan Equity Strategy: Japan Secular Bull Market Arrives: Here is How to Play It (15 Oct 2023) for details of our long-run thesis on Japan.

We reiterate our overweight stance on Japan equities and base-case target price for Topix of 2,800 (+4%) and bull case target of 3,100 (+15%). We model for 15x forward P/E and 9% EPS growth (in JPY) for both 2024 and 2025 in the base case (assuming among other things a level of USDJPY 140 in 4Q24) with upside risk to earnings and multiple in the bull case.

For our Playbook for the equity market post YCC and NIRP adjustment, please see our recent note, Japan Equity Strategy: Japan’s Stock Market Post YCC & NIRP: Investment Playbook (11 Mar 2024). In short, we expect the market will refocus on underlying beneficiaries of the stronger nominal growth environment, including sectors like real estate and construction, as well as consumer companies that have newfound pricing flexibility and strategies (as long as they can manage labour costs through higher productivity). For stock ideas, see our Japan Equity Strategy section.

Added value is based on the sum of OP, personnel expenses, and depreciation expenses; labor productivity based on added value/no. of employee at end-FY

## Navigating Latest Market Dynamics Amid Crowding Risks

Since early February, low-quality growth stocks with high short interest have led the rally, although no sign of recovery has been shown in our Asia Risk Sentiment, suggesting the outperformance has been driven by short covering. On the other hand, momentum stocks have underperformed, and the timing of the reversals are consistent with the observed short covering. Therefore, the recent market dynamics were likely to be dominated by crowding risk management among hedge funds rather than an increase in risk-on sentiment. Our Asia/EM quant strategy team reiterated its neutral stance on Momentum and Value vs. Growth instead of shorting price momentum factor, and prefers a lowvolatility strategy at this stage.

## Asia EM Flows and Positioning

## Kristal Ji

• Aggregate flows (EPFR, to March 13, 2024): EM and China recorded outflows, and active long-only US and EU funds were still selling Chinese equities ( Exhibit 36 ).

• Flows by market (EPFR, to March 13, 2024): Relative to AUM, India, Colombia, and Taiwan recorded the largest inflows within EM, while Brazil, South Korea, and Thailand recorded the largest outflows. ( Exhibit 35 ).

• Japan flows (JPX, to March 8, 2024): Japan foreign inflows have broadly come back since the end of October 2023, having net bought US\$13.8bn YTD 2024. Retailers have been net sellers YTD in aggregate and net sold US\$5.2bn YTD 2024 ( Exhibit 37 and Exhibit 38 ).

• Asia/EM Positioning (EPFR, to January 2024): India underweight in GEM funds allocation widened given managers have not yet caught up with the rising index weight ( Exhibit 3 ). China is seeing dropping benchmark weight and GEM funds managers' allocation, with 91bp underweight vs. benchmark ( Exhibit 4 ). Japan underweight in global long-only equity funds widened to 0.44% as of end-January mainly due to index benchmark weights rising faster than actual portfolio allocation, and we continue to expect Japan to eventually come to overweight ( Exhibit 40 ).

## Asia Quant Strategy

## Gilbert Wong, Jason Ng

## Key Views

• Asia/EM Growth stocks have been outperforming Value stocks since early February; Low Quality Growth with high short interest have led the gains.

• Our Asia Risk Sentiment Indicator shows no sign of recovery, so we believe the recent gains of Low Quality stocks were driven by short covering.

• On the other hand, Momentum stocks turned and are now struggling in APxJ/EM. The timing of these reversals aligns with the short covering observed.

• We believe the recent market dynamics were dominated by crowding risk management among hedge funds, rather than being a sign of risk-on sentiment.

Latest market dynamics: Crowding risk has been in focus among investors since early February. We flagged on February 14, that we think it is time to neutralize long Momentum exposure because of high stock correlations and Momentum factor volatility. Such market dynamics imply difficulty in diversifying risk on both the long and short fronts. In our historical study, unwinding of crowd longs and covering of crowd shorts often happened consequently as investors tended to deleverage. Over the last four weeks, we have seen various signs of crowd trades unwinding in APxJ/EM. For example, Momentum stocks underperformed, Low Quality names with high short interest outperformed ( Exhibit 41 ).

Exhibit 41: Latest factor behavior indicates crowded trades unwinding
![](images/6fe0d0e9aff228730e6bed606842bd633e953150cbee6fe3b47b4fb9b7336b9f.jpg)
Source: Bloomberg, Morgan Stanley Research. Data as of March 8, 2024

Our thoughts: In the last week, US Momentum stocks have started to fade. The reversal occurred only among crowd long names. We are closely monitoring this because historically US Momentum stocks have been highly correlated with Japan Momentum stocks. Meanwhile, Japan Momentum stocks have been the exception in Asia/EM as they are still on the rise, with the six-months rolling factor return approaching extreme territory ( Exhibit 42 ). We believe the seasonality around the Japan dividend ex-date might keep it running into month-end, but the reversal risk in April could be high. Our Japan Quant strategist shared a similar view in his recent publication. Overall, we reiterate our neutral stance on Momentum and Value/Growth. We prefer a Low Volatility strategy at this stage.

Exhibit 42: Mean Reversal Risk of "Momentum Factor" in Japan
![](images/7199892e94e37d89a08341907fa47af8006e7ec8a3779adc82771c2f05b887a6.jpg)
Source: FactSet, Morgan Stanley Research; data coverage: Jan-00 to Mar-24

Risk-off sentiment remains in Asia/EM: According to Morgan Stanley's Asia Risk Sentiment Indicator ( Exhibit 43 ), overall risk allocation among global asset owners continues to prefer low-risk assets as money market funds dominate fund inflows. The sentiment pickup in February, driven by short covering in some low-quality high-beta equities in China, has cooled down into March. Overall risk-off sentiment has been slow moving, not yet hitting extreme territory. Our quant model suggests managing the risk of a momentum reversal by taking a neutral stance, but not to short price momentum factor.

Exhibit 43: Morgan Stanley Asia Risk Sentiment Indicator vs. MSCI EM – As of March 8, 2024
![](images/c4eaf97fdc24b3aa3a970f8540755d0b7ccfe27d6814e0623e0d19ac3a30a66b.jpg)
Source: FactSet, Bloomberg, EPFR, MSCI, Morgan Stanley Research. Note: Buy/Sell signals are generated if average weekly readings of the risk sentiment indicator in the last four weeks were below the 15th percentile or above the 85th percentile. The above red/green lines are tracking the forward 6M returns since the signal appeared. MSARSI methodology and its other details can be referred to in insight #1 in the QuantASIA launch report. Past performance is no guarantee of future results.

## Japan Equity Strategy

Daniel Blake, Sho Nakazawa, Makoto Furukawa, Jonathan Garner, Kristal Ji

## Key Views

• We reiterate our overweight stance on Japan equities and base-case target price for Topix of 2,800 (+4%) and bull-case target of 3,100 (+15%).

• As expected by MS economists and consensus, the Bank of Japan (BOJ) has ended an era of exceptional monetary policy accommodation by removing the Negative Interest Rate Policy, abolishing the formal Yield Curve Control mechanism (while maintaining the ability to make ad hoc bond purchases in the event of a sharp increase in yields) and ceasing the purchase of equity ETFs and REITs.

• Our bullish stance on Japan is supported by the virtuous cycle of rising nominal GDP growth, wages, prices and corporate profits. Overall Shunto wage increase including the seniority portion was +5.28%, much higher than the first tabulation in 2023 and exceeding 5% for the first time in 33 years.

• We expect further inflows from both foreign and retail investors, positioning for stronger nominal growth and corporate governance. Stock purchases through new NISAs marked a new high in January (domestic + overseas stocks) and Sho Nakazawa forecasts NISA inflows to Japan equities to be US\$12bn in 2024.

• Our forecast for JPY appreciation to USD/JPY ¥140 by end-2024 underpins our recommendation for unhedged Japan equity exposure and preference for domestics over exporters

## Catalysts and Signposts:

• April 1: BoJ Tankan Survey (March).

• May: F3/24 results and mid-term plans for some companies

## Japan Focus List

• June: Annual shareholder meetings

## China Equity Strategy

Laura Wang, Catherine Chen, Raymond Liu

## Key Views:

• We remain cautious given downward earnings revision pressure as 4Q23 earnings estimates are being revised down notably. Many Jan-Feb macro data prints surprised on the upside (retail sales, IP, CPI, etc.); however, we think the CNY holiday effect as well as seasonality could explain most of it. Stronger start of the year for the macro could actually lower the chance of further policy step-up in the near term. We see limited upside toward year-end with our latest price targets for China indices: MSCI China at 53 (-4% vs. market closing prices as of March 15, 2024), Hang Seng at 16,000 (-4%) and CSI 300 at 3,500 (-2%). More details here.

• We continue to recommend OW A shares vs. offshore Chinese equities, given manufacturing, industrial and clean energy sectors' skew toward the A-share market. We also see stronger liquidity support for the A-share market, as reflected with the improved Northbound flow and, more important, the strong inflows observed since late Jan-24 into the A-share ETFs, particularly into the large caps (Flows & positioning on A-shares among offshore funds).

• By sector, we continue to prefer select Consumption (with preference for Staples), select Materials and Industrials (given earnings certainty). We continue to UW Properties and Banks.

• Recommend the following alpha generation investment opportunities: 1) Our China/HK Focus List and China A-share Thematic List (see here); and 2) SOE reform (2024 SOE update and FAQs)( Exhibit 47 ).

## Catalysts and Signposts:

• Policy events: Politburo Meeting (late April/late July) and Third Plenum (date TBC).

• Earnings downward revisions are ongoing and should continue though April/early May. Will likely stay as a negative catalyst given the new record negative net-net pre-announcements we have seen for the 4Q23 cycle.

• Macro data prints: CPI and PPI YoY have bottomed, but we think lowflation (a near-zero GDP deflator) is here to stay, as March activity showed soft sequential inflation, and the 0.5ppt expansion in augmented fiscal deficit set by the NPC will likely face downside risk amid continued LGFV and property weakness.

• Liquidity: US 10-year bond yield trend and USDCNY trend. Domestic liquidity could remain relatively tight due to a lack of credit/monetary multiplier effect (China Equity Strategy: China Free Liquidity vs. MSCI China YoY Change [17 Jan 2024]).

## Australia Macro+ Strategy

Chris Nicol, Chris Read, Antony Conte, Isabel Black

## Key Views:

• Our end-2024 price target for the S&P/ASX 200 is 7,350, some 3% below current market levels. Valuations have risen over recent months and are at the top end of their historical relationship with bond yields, which we think puts the emphasis on earnings to drive market upside. While our indicators suggest a trough in earnings is forming, a sharper rebound is not evident at this stage.

• We expect economic growth will remain patchy, and continue to see downside risks across both construction and retail sectors. We do not expect the RBA to pivot to easing as fast as the market expects, and have them on hold through 2024 given sticky services inflation, less-restrictive policy settings and further fiscal stimulus emerging.

• Our recommended sector positioning remains relatively defensive: overweight energy, healthcare and insurance as well as select quality growth and offshore earners names. UW domestic cycle exposure including banks, consumer and housing-linked.

## Catalysts and Signposts:

• February Reporting Season: We observed a high level of dispersion in sector returns in February as the market continued to set new highs at 7,699, +0.9% higher m/m. Technology and Discretionary sectors were strong, Financials added value, whilst Resources were a drag on performance.

• RBA March Meeting: RBA evolution remains key to the domestic cycle, given the sensitivity of the economy to changes in rates. We continue to see a cautious pivot from the RBA.

• Government May Budget: Government policy remains very active, and we see scope for further policy announcements on both the spending and tax sides into the May Budget.

## Corporate Catalysts:

• March 2024, S&P/ASX Quarterly Index Review: On Friday, March 1, 2024, S&P Dow Jones Indices announced changes in the S&P/ASX Indices. This review included the S&P/ASX 20, 50, 100, 200 and 300 Indices along with the All Ordinaries Index, effectively the entire S&P/ASX Index hierarchy was reviewed. Changes are effective close of trading on Friday, March 15, 2024. At this review, we observed a number of changes across the indices including:

QBE replacing NEM within the S&P/ASX 20 Index.

Two changes within the S&P/ASX 100 Index - PME and FLT replacing AWC and RGN.

Within the S&P/ASX 200 Index we observed four changes, with AD8, RED, SDR, SMR replacing CHN, CXO, SYA and WBT.

<sub>°</sub> Across the S&P/ASX 300 Index, there were 14 additions and 10 deletions, to bring the index constituent membership back to 300. Additions include: BMN, DVP, LOT, LRS, MEI, MRM, MVF, NXG, PDI, PFP, RDX, REG, TUA and WC8. Deletions include: AGY, APX, BCB, LKE, LLL, NMT, PBH, SSR, SVR and SWM.

## India Equity Strategy

Ridham Desai, Sheela Rathi, Nayant Parekh

## Key Views:

• India is our key pick across EM markets: Fundamentals are underscored by strong macro stability as a result of improving terms of trade, flexible inflation targeting and stable non-portfolio foreign flows, earnings growth of about 20% annually over the next three to four years led by an emerging private capex cycle, re-leveraging of corporate balance sheets, and unfolding of a structural rise in discretionary consumption and a reliable source of domestic risk capital. These factors have reduced both correlations and volatility of Indian stocks relative to EM.

• Base-case outlook is for an improving absolute return and outperformance to EM: We expect equities to rise into the 2024 general elections, as the market is likely to price in continuity and a majority government. While we are in a macro market (in contrast to a stock-pickers' market), the macro trade could peak in 2024.

## Catalysts and Signposts:

• Politics – Actions from the opposition alliance, which create a view that it is becoming a credible contender to the incumbent.

• US stock and bond market cues.

• Oil Prices – While India remains less affected than in the past, a further rise would present headwinds.

• Earnings Strength – Our base case is 7% and 10% ahead of consensus on F2025 and F2026, respectively.

• Monetary Policy – Shallow rate cuts but Fed moves remain uncertain.

• Bond flows further improving the structure of the BoP.

• Rise in net issuances that could moderate the continuing strong domestic bid.

## Sector Allocation

• Cyclicals > Defensives

• Large-caps > SMID-caps

• OW Financials, Consumer Discretionary, Industrials and Technology

• UW other sectors

## South Korea Equity Strategy

Joon Seok, Heewon Choi, Michelle Kim

## Key Views:

• Less than 30 Days left for the 22nd National Assembly elections; range-bound in the near term: The market is waiting to see the results of the 22nd National Assembly election to be held on April 10, and until then we believe that the market should remain range-bound with external factors to matter more during this period. We stick to our sector allocation leaning to mid-cycle beneficiaries with a corporate reform twist – Tech, Industrials and Banks.

• The Rise of the Banks? We upgraded Hana Financial (HFG, 086790.KS) and Shinhan Finance (SFG, 055550.KS) to Overweight, and tactically downgraded KB Financial (KFG, 105560.KS) and Kakao Bank (KKB, 323410.KS) to Equal-weight. We also upgraded Industrial Bank of Korea (IBKm 024110.KS) to Equal-weight (The Rise of the Banks?). The previous reform-driven rally of Korean banks was in 2016- 17, with its forward P/B reaching 0.6x in 2017 vs. current level of 0.4x. This implies 50% upside if we track back to 2017. To achieve this, the following three things need to happen: 1) easing regulatory backdrop; 2) faster progress of Value-Up Program; and 3) improvements in non-banking businesses.

• EV Batteries – Not out of the woods yet: Our Materials analyst, Young Suk Shin, noted that market share of Korean EV batteries in Europe dropped at the expense of Chinese suppliers, and he believes this trend could continue in the next few years. Morgan Stanley's estimates remain \~25% below consensus on 2024 operating profit (W2.3trn MSe vs W3.1trn consensus), as the team sees additional downside risks to 2Q24/2H24 consensus estimates.

## Catalysts and Signposts:

• AGM Season (mid- to late March): Focus will be on companies with shareholder proposals from investors.

• General Election – April 10, 2024: Post-election policy direction could be affected by elections results, and we are waiting for more clarity on policy pledges from the political parties.

• BoK Outlook: We continue to expect BOK to begin rate cuts in 3Q24 (BoK Review: On the Way to a Dovish Move)

• Other catalysts: WGBI Global Bond Index inclusion announcement, MSCI watchlist announcement and lifting of short-sell ban.

## Taiwan Equity Strategy

Terence Cheng, Jenny Ting

## Key Views:

• We continue to see a robust TAIEX performance YTD in 2024, given intact fundamentals for exporters with rising visibility after end-market inventory destocking cycles for multiple sectors (both Tech and Non-Tech) concluded with continually increasing trading volume, indicating abundant liquidity. Despite likely near-term volatility into earnings season, a leading cash dividend yield vs. regional peers could still offer some downside support to TAIEX valuation, in our view.

• Our Dec-24 TAIEX target is at 18,500 with a bull-case target at 20,000: TAIEX is now trading above our year-end index target, thanks mainly to positive sentiment on the Tech sector spilling over from the US market and market expectations on global AI investment.

• Foreign investors’ participation has also been on the rise with foreign investors holdings of TAIEX up by 2ppt YTD in 2024 (compared with +1.14ppt YoY in 2023, an acceleration of capital inflow to TAIEX).

• Key Risks: We would be mindful about overshooting of the TAIEX in the near term as the US election becomes more of a focus by investors in 2H24.

## Catalysts and Signposts:

• Foreign investors participate more actively or add net positions on the TAIEX.

• Fed and CBC monetary policy outlook – MS forecasts 100bp Fed cuts in 2024 starting June, with CBC on hold until early 2025.

## Sector Allocation:

• OW: Information Technology, Consumer Discretionary, Industrials

• EW: Financials, Consumer Staples

• UW: Materials and Telecoms

# Singapore and ASEAN Equity Strategy

Wilson Ng, Daniel Blake, Kristal Ji, Aitong Li

## Key Views:

• Singapore: Maintain OW on Singapore given high dividend yields and relatively low risks from global slowdown spillovers, with historical outperformance in EM bear markets. 12mf P/E currently stands at 11.7x, 1.5x lower than its 10-year average.

• Indonesia: An unofficial quick count of votes has indicated victory by Prabowo-Gibran (official results to be released, March 20), which is a positive development vs. our previous concerns of a drawn-out scenario. We will still need to monitor the cabinet formation process and initial policy priorities, but our sector analysts view the election outcome as encouraging for investment sentiment. See Indonesia: Beyond Elections – How to Position? (14 Feb 2024) for more details. We remain OW Indonesia Financials, while the overall MSCI Indonesia market ranks 7th in our APxJ/EM framework. Indonesia's valuations are trading slightly higher than their usually (modest) premiums to EM, while relative profitability is in line with historical averages (also a slight premium). EPFR data shows investor positioning is OW Indonesia.

• Philippines: Macro risks persist with a high current account deficit, while foreign participation remains subdued. We recommend cherry picking and our ASEAN Property analyst Wilson Ng prefers Philippines developers, especially Ayala Land (see ASEAN Property: 2024 Outlook: Watch for falling rates (10 Jan 2024) and ASEAN Multi-Industry: Philippine Titans: Ayala and SM (20 Aug 2023)).

## Latin America Equity Strategy

Nikolaj Lippmann, Juan Ayala, Julia Nogueira

## Key Views:

• In Latin America, we lean into four key secular themes that can drive superior EPS growth: 1) digitalization (Nu Holdings, NU.N; Mercadolibre Inc., MEL.N; Globant SA, GLOB.N; Totvs S.A., TOTS3.SA); 2) near-shoring (Grupo Financiero Banorte, GFNORTEO.MX; Inmobiliaria Vesta, VESTA.MX; Prologis Property Mexico, FIBRAPL14.MX); 3) energizing the world (Petrobras, PBR.N; Petro Rio, PRIO3.SA; Enel Chile, ENELCHILE.SN); and 4) expansion of Brazil's agricultural frontiers (Rumo, RAIL3.SA).

• We are overweight Mexico and Brazil: In Mexico, the near-shoring trend should continue to affect manufacturing growth, creating a demand shock for labor, electricity and manufacturing facilities that in turn will elevate prices and incentivize new investments. In Brazil, we see a very strong case for a longer-term external transformation via the expansion of both its energy and agricultural frontiers, which could add 22-30% to gross exports by the end of the decade, according to our analysts. However, today these are better micro stock-picking themes than macro ones.

• We have a quality bias in our Brazil and Latin America model portfolios: As we worry about the tail risks of higher-for-longer rates in the US and a potential hardlanding scenario in China, we prefer to lean into quality names.

• We expect local and international investors to wait for three factors before turning more bullish Brazilian equities: 1) A clear path toward single-digit rates; 2) signs of economic re-acceleration; and 3) clarity on tail risks both from within (interventionism) and without (China). The latter could be a risk to the commodity outlook and thus to the Brazil base- and bull-case scenarios.

• We worry that a potential delay on the start of an easing cycle in the US could be a headwind for the pace of further interest rate cuts across the region: Our US economist Ellen Zentner believes the Fed should deliver its first rate cut in June (for details see FOMC Preview: Not Much Has Changed).

• Finally, we are underweight Chile (current account imbalances and high China exposure), Peru (lingering political uncertainty coupled with China exposure), and Colombia (heterodox policy mix).

## Catalysts and Signposts:

• Brazil government 2025 budget guidelines (mid-April 2024): We believe that if the government proposes a downward revision of the country's fiscal target from the current primary deficit of 0.0% of GDP, it could be a signal to markets a looser fiscal policy from the Executive, which could further delay the progression and magnitude of the current easing cycle.

• Mexico Presidential elections (June 2, 2024): We believe investors will focus on three key factors: 1) energy policy (electricity sector reform and cleanup, and the future vision and priorities around oil company Pemex); 2) fiscal policy (Mexico has been an emerging market standout in recent years, with some late slippage in 2024); and 3) the institutional framework in Mexico that represents.

Morgan Stanley and its affiliates do business that relates to companies/instruments covered in Morgan Stanley Research, including market making, providing liquidity, fund management, commercial banking, extension of credit, investment services and investment banking. Morgan Stanley sells to and buys from customers the securities/instruments of companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research on a principal basis. Morgan Stanley may have a position in the debt of the Company or instruments discussed in this report. ## STOCK RATINGS

Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy, Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell. Investors should carefully read the definitions of al ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone. In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice. An investor's decision to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

## Global Stock Ratings Distribution

## (as of February 29, 2024)

The Stock Ratings described below apply to Morgan Stanley's Fundamental Equity Research and do not apply to Debt Research produced by the Firm.

For disclosure purposes only (in accordance with FINRA requirements), we include the category headings of Buy, Hold, and Sell alongside our ratings of Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight. Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy, Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold, and sell but represent recommended relative weightings (see definitions below). To satisfy regulatory requirements, we correspond Overweight, our most positive stock rating, with a buy recommendation; we correspond Equal-weight and Not-Rated to hold and Underweight to sell recommendations, respectively.

## Analyst Stock Ratings

Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.

## Analyst Industry Views

Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below. Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below. Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index

## Important Disclosures for Morgan Stanley Smith Barney LLC & E\*TRADE Securities LLC Customers

Important disclosures regarding the relationship between the companies that are the subject of Morgan Stanley Research and Morgan Stanley Smith Barney LLC or Morgan Stanley or any of their affiliates, are available on the Morgan Stanley Wealth Management disclosure website at www.morganstanley.com/online/researchdisclosures. For Morgan Stanley specific disclosures, you may refer to www.morganstanley.com/researchdisclosures.

Each Morgan Stanley research report is reviewed and approved on behalf of Morgan Stanley Smith Barney LLC and E\*TRADE Securities LLC. This review and approval is conducted by the same person who reviews the research report on behalf of Morgan Stanley. This could create a conflict of interest.

## Other Important Disclosures

Morgan Stanley & Co. International PLC and its affiliates have a significant financial interest in the debt securities of America Movil SAB de CV, Banco Bradesco, Banco do Brasil, Cemex, DBS Group Holdings, ICICI Bank, Itochu, KT Corp, LG Chem, Mercadolibre Inc., Mitsubishi Heavy Industries, Petrobras, QBE Insurance Group, Recruit Holdings, Reliance Industries, Renesas Electronics, Samsonite International, Singapore Telecom, SK hynix, SK Telecom Co Ltd, Sumitomo Mitsui FG, Sumitomo Realty & Dev., Telstra Corporation, TSMC, United Overseas Bank, Vale, ZTO Express. Morgan Stanley Research policy is to update research reports as and when the Research Analyst and Research Management deem appropriate, based on developments with the issuer, the sector, or the market that may have a material impact on the research views or opinions stated therein. In addition, certain Research publications are intended to be updated on a regular periodic basis (weekly/monthly/quarterly/annual) and will ordinarily be updated with that frequency, unless the Research Analyst and Research Management determine that a different publication schedule is appropriate based on current conditions.

Morgan Stanley is not acting as a municipal advisor and the opinions or views contained herein are not intended to be, and do not constitute, advice within the meaning of Section 975 of the Dodd-Frank Wall Street Reform and Consumer Protection Act

Morgan Stanley produces an equity research product called a "Tactical Idea." Views contained in a "Tactical Idea" on a particular stock may be contrary to the recommendations or views expressed in research on the same stock. This may be the result of differing time horizons, methodologies, market events, or other factors. For all research available on a particular stock, please contact your sales representative or go to Matrix at http://www.morganstanley.com/matrix

Morgan Stanley Research is provided to our clients through our proprietary research portal on Matrix and also distributed electronically by Morgan Stanley to clients.
